Profound / content optimizationExploratory brief · 01 Oct 2026
Dataset → evidence → content

What makes a page
worth citing?

Query alignment is a promising starting hypothesis. Broader patterns favor more substantive, structured pages—but the strongest comparisons are limited by how the dataset was sampled.

A briefing for choosing a lightweight content-generation demo, with source rows you can inspect yourself.

The askDatasetFindingsSame-query checkSample rowsBrainstorm
9,700citation records
970hostnames · 5 top + 5 bottom each
9,527distinct exact URLs
29eligible same-query groups after filters
01 / Objective

Discover a pattern. Apply it to a draft.

What the brief asks for

Analyze HTML and content features associated with stronger citation performance, then build a lightweight tool that generates or improves content for a target query. Deliver a complete example and explain the next steps.

What this dataset can support

Exploratory associations among already-cited pages. “Top” and “bottom” are relative to each hostname. There are no citation counts, exposure denominators, engine identifiers, or observation dates to estimate citation probability or uplift.

02 / Data inventory

Balanced by hostname. Rarely matched by query.

Five parquet files occupy 617 MB on disk. The original 500 MB ZIP remains in Downloads and passed its integrity check. Each column is a string; the promised separate cleaned Markdown column is absent.

¹ Exact stored-string cardinality, including the empty string. There are 9,011 distinct nonblank prompts. Nonempty payloads can still be failed scrapes.

Data quality

  • 91 blank prompts: 70 top, 21 bottom.
  • 28 excess exact duplicate rows.
  • 14 URLs carry both labels, involving 76 rows.
  • 185 failure candidates: 69 top, 116 bottom, based on text heuristics.
  • 58 Markdown-like payloads inside html_content; 64 rows lack recognized HTML markup.

Comparability

  • 920 / 970 hosts share no normalized nonblank query across labels.
  • 74 shared host/query groups; only 42 have different URLs across labels.
  • 36 remain after basic quality and conflict checks.
  • 29 remain with the additional extracted-text threshold.
  • Top rows include 143 homepages versus 22 bottom; page purpose is a potential confounder.

Exact wording is a limited matching rule. Unicode normalization, case folding, and whitespace collapse preserve punctuation. Paraphrases may share intent without matching; even identical queries do not establish equivalent exposure or scrape timing.

03 / Broad associations

Alignment and structure survive basic quality filters.

The clean analysis retains 8,687 unique pages, with both labels available in 931 hosts. It excludes conflicting labels, URLs with any failure/sparse/non-HTML flag, and pages below 100 extracted words. Each host is weighted equally in the reported differences.

FeatureMedian host differenceTop higher / lower / tied
Title query-token coverage+7.64 pp651 / 275 / 5
Body query-token coverage+7.83 pp633 / 294 / 4
Heading count+4.0656 / 263 / 12
Extracted words+214.4602 / 326 / 3

For each host: mean feature value of top pages minus mean of bottom pages. The table reports the median of these host differences. “pp” means percentage points. Query coverage is literal token overlap, not semantic relevance.

Use these as hypotheses, not quotas. Adding 214 words or four headings is not an evidence-backed recipe. Heading count partly reflects length. Similar directions appear on editorial-looking paths, but heading density is weaker there; lists and structured data offer no universal prescription.

Top prompts are shorter and more often include English comparison markers (34.2% versus 23.5%). Different queries, page purposes, languages, shared templates, and residual boilerplate can influence these results.

04 / Stricter sensitivity

When the question is held constant, certainty shrinks.

29 host/query groups · 27 hosts · 66 unique pages. Page differences are calculated within query, then averaged within hostname. This is a small, selected descriptive subset—not held-out validation.

Top higherBottom higherTied
Title query-token coverage

FeatureTop higher / lower / tiedMedian host difference
Title query-token coverage13 / 1 / 130
Body query-token coverage8 / 10 / 90
Heading count14 / 8 / 5+1
Extracted words15 / 12 / 0+43

Title alignment still looks worth testing: 13 hosts favor top pages, one favors bottom, and 13 tie. Body overlap and length are less consistent here. Table presence favors top in one host, bottom in two, and ties in 24. None of these comparisons establishes that an edit causes more citations.

05 / Eyeball the data

20 source rows, with the context intact.

Six curated rows form three matched pairs. Six illustrate blank prompts, languages, and mixed payload formats. Eight are a reproducible hash sample—four per label. This is an inspection set, not a representative estimate of dataset prevalence.

Metrics below are extracted from the exact sampled row, not the longest snapshot selected in the aggregate analysis. Raw payloads are escaped as text and never executed. Website links open live URLs; the excerpts are supplied snapshots.

SAP Data Replication SAP on AWS – The Fundamentals If you are planning to move SAP on AWS or need reasons and information to do so, this blog is for you. Learn why SAP implementations on AWS make sense and how it helps you get a better return on your SAP investments. Learn the SAP AWS architectural patterns, and methods to get your data to AWS. Also learn how the BryteFlow SAP Data Lake Builder automates your journey effortlessly. Quick Links Why SAP on AWS makes sense Methods of SAP on AWS Deployment SAP on AWS Architecture Advantages of moving SAP on AWS How to get data from SAP to AWS Mechanisms for SAP Data Extraction SAP Data Extraction Tools Highlights of the BryteFlow SAP Data Lake Builder Why SAP on AWS makes sense Your on-premise SAP implementations have performed well in the past, but of late they may be falling short in terms of agility, maintenance and IT costs. In recent years the Cloud has become the place to be – for faster insights, reduced infrastructure costs, and flexibility of operations. Increasingly on-premise SAP ERP systems are making way for Cloud-based SAP ERP systems. And when it comes to Cloud, AWS is a natural choice to deploy your SAP systems. How to Carry Out a Successful SAP Cloud Migration According to a survey conducted by IDG Research, companies running SAP on AWS were extremely satisfied and intended to move even more SAP workloads to the Cloud. The satisfaction level was seen to increase with their duration on AWS. 96% of customers reported a reduction in TCO (total cost of ownership) saving 26% in overall costs on average. However long-term SAP on AWS customers reported benefits such as an increase in business efficiencies – in areas like time-to-market, innovation and customer service. SAP Data Extraction: Useful Tools You Need to Know SAP and AWS, a time-honored partnership Since 2011, AWS has been collaborating with SAP to migrate SAP systems and applications to AWS Cloud. SAP supports most SAP applications on AWS. As an AWS customer you can get on-demand access to whatever you need, be it compute power, additional storage, servers or networking. The AWS platform is self-service, and you need to pay only for the resources you use. Provisioning resources can be done with just a few clicks – no expensive hardware to buy – leading to overall cost-effectiveness. RISE with SAP (Everything You Need to Know) Amazon EC2 instances are tested and SAP-certified SAP being a unique software, has special requirements. AWS has coordinated with SAP to test and certify Amazon EC2 instances for SAP on AWS implementations. An Amazon EC2 instance is a virtual server on the AWS cloud platform and is part of the AWS Elastic Compute Cloud (EC2). Earlier versions of Amazon EC2 instances for SAP HANA are fully supported and have the same functionality and features. You can run non-production workloads on both current and earlier Amazon EC2 instance types, which is convenient for customers. Who provides the most effective runtime defense for workloads?

Top 5 CWPP (Cloud Workload Protection Platforms) In 2025

Top 5 CWPP Vendors for Workload Defense [2025 Review] Atharva Shah |  May 26, 2025 This post reviews the capabilities of leading Cloud Workload Protection Platforms (CWPPs) for 2025. It covers essential features, outlines top vendors, and discusses critical selection criteria for CWPP solutions. Reading Time: 7 minutes Table of Contents Essential CWPP Features Quick Overview of CWPP Vendors 2025 Top 5 Cloud Workload Protection Platforms (CWPP) 1. AccuKnox CWPP 2. Palo Alto Networks Prisma Cloud 3. CrowdStrike Cloud Security 4. Aqua Security Platform 5. Wiz Important Considerations When Choosing a CWPP Conclusion FAQs TL;DR CWPP vendors provide runtime protection for cloud workloads —including VMs, containers, and serverless functions—ensuring visibility, compliance, and threat mitigation across hybrid and multi-cloud infrastructures. Top CWPP vendors in 2025 include AccuKnox, Palo Alto Prisma Cloud, CrowdStrike, Aqua Security, and Wiz , each offering distinct capabilities in threat detection, microsegmentation, and compliance enforcement. AccuKnox CWPP stands out with eBPF-native runtime security, zero trust policy enforcement, and strong Kubernetes protection , making it ideal for cloud-native and DevSecOps-driven environments. Key CWPP features to prioritize include microsegmentation, behavioral policy discovery, vulnerability scanning, and regulatory compliance alignment with standards like NIST, MITRE ATT&CK, and PCI DSS. Choosing the right CWPP vendor depends on the deployment model (agentless vs. agent-based), integration support, scalability, and security posture needs , with AccuKnox offering flexible deployment and deep runtime control. AccuKnox CWPP—Cloud Workload Protection Platform is a security solution that protects workloads across physical servers, VMs, containers, and serverless environments in hybrid and multi-cloud infrastructures. According to Gartner, CWPPs offer consistent visibility and control, regardless of workload location. AccuKnox’s CWPP offers inline mitigation using Linux Security Modules (AppArmor, SELinux, BPF-LSM). Unlike traditional post-attack responses that act only after a threat is executed, KubeArmor blocks malicious processes before they can run, preventing remote code execution and ensuring real-time protection for cloud-native workloads. Let us see the top CWPP vendors dominating the market in 2025. Essential CWPP Features Effective CWPPs protect diverse workloads, from VMs to containers and serverless functions. Key features include: Runtime Security with eBPF: Leverages eBPF and BPF LSM for granular, low-level observability and control. Enables inline mitigation of zero-day threats at the kernel level without source modification. Ensures consistent security posture with minimal performance impact. Benefit: Reduces exposure to new vulnerabilities and prevents unauthorized actions in real time. Application & Pod Security: Enforces deep runtime policies for applications and pods, particularly in Kubernete [Excerpt truncated at 3,000 characters]

# Page 1 According to surveys of working adults and IT professionals conducted in 2022, almost 7 in 10 (70%) respondents reported having encountered vishing attacks. This represents an increase from 54% in 2020. Source: Statista March 2023 cofense.com/vishing OVERVIEW Vishing is a prevalent and serious threat that lets adversaries gain access to your company’s enterprise and direct access to your most critical data. Today’s sophisticated vishing techniques easily bypass many organizations’ prevention tactics, making vishing a rewarding option for attackers. As a result, security teams must understand how vishing targets their organization, the potential impacts on the business, and how to update their anti- phishing strategies. According to the FBI, vishing has seen a significant increase since the beginning of the COVID-19 pandemic and employees, both in the office and working in other locations, are at a higher risk of a vishing attack than they were two years ago. W HAT IS THE DIFFERENCE BETWEEN PHISHING, SMISHING, AND VISHING? While each type of attack attempts to steal personal information, each takes a different approach to accomplish the goal. Phishing uses emails and links, smishing uses text messages or common messaging apps, and vishing uses voice calls and voicemails to obtain sensitive information. Let’s take a closer look at vishing and how Cofense can help you prepare your employees to identify and report malicious vishing campaigns. ANATOMY OF A VISHING ATTACK Vishing attacks usually start with contact by phone, email, and/or text message and request an employee to reach out via a voice call to request personal or financial information. Often, callers pretend to be from the company’s IT or finance department; impersonate an executive in their respective company, business partner, or federal agency; and/or claim to be from a software vendor used by employees at your organization. The caller attempts to convince the employee to provide private information or take action that will be used to compromise the company’s networks, gain access, or steal data or funds. Users should be suspicious of anyone who calls with a request that includes: • Account issues demanding urgency or immediate resolution of a problem that is difficult to verify (IT access/failure, financial accounts, locked-out logins, etc.). • Requests for information like full name, birth date, social security number, etc. Threat actors may even provide partial information on coworkers or vendors to convince victims to release the information they need. RESPOND AND RECOVER FROM VISHING ATTACKS Vishing sets the groundwork to launch more in-depth attacks which can lead to more substantial crimes, including malware, ransomware, data and financial theft, malicious data deletion, and general business disruption. Through vishing, hackers can even gain unrestricted access to your entire network, placing all the organization’s dat [Excerpt truncated at 3,000 characters]

# Page 1
According to  surveys of working  adults and IT  professionals  conducted in 2022,  almost 7 in 10  (70%) respondents  reported having  encountered  vishing attacks.  This represents an  increase from 54%  in 2020.  Source: Statista March 2023  cofense.com/vishing  OVERVIEW  Vishing is a prevalent and serious threat that lets adversaries gain access to your  company’s enterprise and direct access to your most critical data. Today’s sophisticated  vishing techniques easily bypass many organizations’ prevention tactics, making vishing a  rewarding option for attackers. As a result, security teams must understand how vishing  targets their organization, the potential impacts on the business, and how to update their anti-  phishing strategies.  According to the FBI, vishing has seen a significant increase since the beginning  of the COVID-19 pandemic and employees, both in the office and working in other  locations, are at a higher risk of a vishing attack than they were two years ago.  W HAT IS THE DIFFERENCE BETWEEN PHISHING, SMISHING, AND VISHING?  While each type of attack attempts to steal personal information, each takes a  different approach to accomplish the goal. Phishing uses emails and links, smishing  uses text messages or common messaging apps, and vishing uses voice calls and voicemails  to obtain sensitive information. Let’s take a closer look at vishing and how Cofense can help  you prepare your employees to identify and report malicious vishing campaigns.  ANATOMY OF A VISHING ATTACK  Vishing attacks usually start with contact by phone, email, and/or text message and request  an employee to reach out via a voice call to request personal or financial information. Often,  callers pretend to be from the company’s IT or finance department; impersonate an executive  in their respective company, business partner, or federal agency; and/or claim to be from a  software vendor used by employees at your organization. The caller attempts to convince the  employee to provide private information or take action that will be used to compromise the  company’s networks, gain access, or steal data or funds.  Users should be suspicious of anyone who calls with a request that includes:  •   Account issues demanding urgency or immediate resolution of a problem that is difficult to  verify (IT access/failure, financial accounts, locked-out logins, etc.).  •   Requests for information like full name, birth date, social security number, etc.  Threat actors may even provide partial information on coworkers or vendors to convince  victims to release the information they need.  RESPOND AND RECOVER FROM VISHING ATTACKS  Vishing sets the groundwork to launch more in-depth attacks which can lead to more  substantial crimes, including malware, ransomware, data and financial theft, malicious  data deletion, and general business disruption. Through vishing, hackers can even gain  unrestricted access to your entire network, placing all the organization’s data at risk.  Vishing and related phishing incidents must be reported when recognized so security teams  can move quickly to respond.  Vishing Attack Prevention  and Response
# Page 2
cofense.com/vishing  MINIMIZE THE IMPACT OF VISHING ATTACKS  A solid security response plan that minimizes the potential impact of a vishing attack on your business must include:  •   An easy, non-punitive, and efficient way for users to immediately report incidents that include the information disclosed, to  determine potential damage of the attack.  •   Documented procedures to quickly alert financial institutions, vendors, and federal agencies, along with any partners or  branches of the business that may be affected.  •   A way to communicate the security threat so your enterprise is aware and can recognize other potential risks.  STRENGTHEN YOUR SECURITY POSTURE WITH CUSTOMIZED VISHING ENGAGEMENT  With Cofense, you can take your program even further to help maximize your return on investment. Our professional services  team can develop and execute a customized vishing program specifically tailored to your organization's needs on top of what  you get through our vishing learning modules. Let’s take a closer look at the Vishing Simulation program.  PhishMe and Reporter are required for Cofense's Vishing Simulation program. The program includes:  •   A vishing outreach program layered on top of the annual phishing program.  •   Quarterly vishing simulations conducted on employees.  •   Upon reaching the interactive voice response (IVR), employees will be informed that the simulation is part of a larger  company-sanctioned security awareness training program.  •   Susceptible employees receive additional training content to reinforce preferred behavior/action.  •   Localized program execution by region.  •   Robust post-simulation reporting provided regarding employees who fell susceptible to the training.  COFENSE LMS VISHING MODULES INCLUDE:  •   Micro Vishing   – An overview of vishing including pretexting, emotional appeals, priming the recipient, and what to  do to avoid the traps.  •   Vishing Simulators   – Our latest vishing simulation includes new threats to detect, updated phone choices, and  customization options to improve immersion.  •   Vishing Comprehensive Simulator   – Helps the user apply critical thinking to voice mail messages.  THE COFENSE WAY TO PREVENT VISHING ATTACKS  Vishing can often go undetected, and security awareness is critical because untrained employees may not recognize  and report suspicious phone calls. Vishing attacks focus on high-value business targets, such as call centers, IT  administrators, accounts payable, HR, and more. Security teams should implement the following vishing prevention  measures to protect their organization:  •   Update security awareness programs with Cofense’ s vishing LMS modules to train employees to recognize and  report vishing attempts.  •   Add Cofense Reporter to your email task bar for easy and efficient reporting.  •   Vish your employees at a regular cadence using vishing simulations  •   Train employees at all levels to verify caller identity and the authenticity of email links.  •   Advise all employees to refrain from texting sensitive information in social and messaging apps other than official  company platforms.  •   Use your enterprise technology stack to block unknown numbers and add mobile apps to route calls to your  company’s VoIP.  •   Make sure anti-spam and anti-phishing solutions are current.  •   Augment native email security with Cofense threat detection and leverage auto-quarantine to remediate  suspicious emails.

# Page 8
8  Executive Summary  As battery electric vehicles (BEVs) continue to grow in number on European  roads, their integration into urban infrastructure — particularly in covered parking  facilities — has become a key focus for regulatory and safety considerations.  Recognizing this, the   European Commission’s Sustainable Transport Forum  (STF)   Task   Force   6   has   developed   comprehensive   guidelines   aimed   at  identifying and mitigating fire risks, as well as ensuring the safe installation of  recharging infrastructure in covered parking areas. Thi s guidance, which provides  an overview of technical, organizational, structural, and preventive fire safety  measures, is intended for public authorities and other stakeholders involved in  fire   safety   in   covered   parking   facilities.   These   include   parking   ope rators,  designers, risk assessors, fire service organizations, and original equipment  manufacturers (OEMs).  Despite initial concerns, existing studies show that BEVs do not present a higher  fire   risk   than   internal   combustion   engine   vehicles   (ICEVs).   However,   fires  involving   lithium - ion   batteries   present   unique   challenges,   such   as   thermal  runaway, jet fires, and   vapor cloud explosions. Additionally, fire safety issues may  arise from the condition and reliability of recharging infrastructure.  The guidance outlines several fire safety strategies aimed at reducing fire risks in  covered parking facilities, which can be grouped into five key areas:  1.   Prevention : Recommends fire risk assessments for both new and existing  parking facilities.  2.   Detection : Suggests the installation of advanced fire detection systems  that can quickly identify fires involving BEVs.  3.   Evacuation : Stresses the importance of designing clear evacuation routes  and placing recharging points away from emergency exits to facilitate  quick evacuations.  4.   Propagation Control : Recommends using fire - resistant systems and  compartmentalization in parking structures to prevent the spread of fire  between vehicles.  5.   Firefighting : Emphasizes the need for specialized firefighting equipment  and techniques tailored to the unique risks posed by BEV fires.  In conclusion, these guidelines provide a crucial roadmap for addressing fire  safety challenges in covered parking facilities where BEVs are present, while also  supporting EU decarbonization goals.   Hence ,   the guidance   a

What features should finance teams consider for corporate cards?

Modern corporate cards: Features to look for | FleetOwner

https://www.fleetowner.com/home/blog/55092932/modern-corporate-cards-features-to-look-for

43415949 | Kondratova | Dreamstime.com Modern corporate cards offer a wide range of features designed to help streamline expense management, enhance security, and provide valuable insights into company spending. These cards have evolved far beyond simple payment tools, incorporating technology to offer solutions that align with the needs of modern fleets and businesses. Here’s a breakdown of essential features to look for in modern corporate cards: 1. Digital access and management Look for corporate cards that offer robust digital platforms and mobile apps with real-time expense tracking, categorization, and reporting. These tools should enable administrators to quickly review transactions, approve expenses, and manage cardholder accounts from anywhere, fostering transparency and efficiency in expense management. 2. Robust spending controls and transaction controls Modern corporate cards should provide the ability to set customizable spending limits and controls at both the card and account levels. This feature allows businesses to enforce budget constraints, manage cash flow more effectively, and prevent unauthorized spending. Admins should be able to adjust these settings in real-time based on evolving business needs. Sponsored Recommendations The all-new Volvo VNL - Build Yours Online Today Volvo Blue contract adaptive maintenance VNL 840 - Our premium midroof sleeper VNL 660 - Checking all the boxes 3. Integration with accounting software One of the most valuable features of modern corporate cards is the ability to integrate seamlessly with existing accounting and expense management systems. This integration simplifies the reconciliation process, reduces manual data entry, and ensures accuracy in financial reporting, saving time and reducing errors. 4. Advanced security features Security features are crucial for finance teams. Modern corporate cards have advanced security features such as EMV chips, biometric verification, and real-time fraud monitoring alerts. Additionally, the ability to instantly freeze and unfreeze cards via an app provides an extra layer of security against unauthorized use. 5. Virtual cards Virtual cards and single-use numbers offer a secure and flexible way to manage online transactions and subscriptions. These features protect against fraud by generating unique card numbers for each transaction or vendor, reducing the risk of data breaches and unauthorized charges. See also: New card locking tool helps prevent fuel fraud 6. Rewards and incentives While not the primary reason to choose a corporate card, rewards, and incentive programs can provide significant added value. Look for cards that offer cash back, points, or travel rewards based on spending. 7. Global acceptance and currency flexibility For businesses with international operations or those that travel frequently, it's important to select a corporate card that offers wide global acceptance and competitive foreign transaction fees. Some cards also provide curre [Excerpt truncated at 3,000 characters]

Home Website Design Insite Blog 5 Reasons Your Website Is Slow and How to Fix It Scott Langdon June 11, 2024 Summarize this article in: Key Takeaways Slow load times can result in higher bounce rates, lower search engine rankings, and lost revenue. A fast-loading website improves user experience by reducing frustration and increasing engagement, with Google recommending pages load within 2.5 seconds. Websites with faster load times have a better chance of ranking higher in Google search results, as page speed has been a ranking factor since 2018. A slow website is more than just a minor inconvenience for users. It can have significant negative impacts on your business. Slow load times can lead to higher bounce rates, lower search engine rankings , and lost revenue. This post will explain the most common causes of slow-loading websites and how to fix them. But first, let’s take a closer look at why optimizing site speed is so important. Why Website Speed Matters Here are the three main reasons you should prioritize your website’s page speed. Improved User Experience A fast-loading website provides a seamless and satisfying experience for your visitors. In an age where users expect instant access to information, any delay in load times can lead to user frustration and disengagement. In fact, according to Google , the likelihood of visitors bouncing from your site increases 32% as your page load time goes from one to three seconds. So, how long should it take for your pages to load? Google’s latest documentation says pages should load within 2.5 seconds to provide a good user experience. Anything longer than four seconds is considered too slow. Better Search Engine Rankings Faster-loading websites have a better chance of ranking prominently in Google’s search results. Since 2018 , Google has officially used page speed as a ranking factor for desktop and mobile searches. Google aims to provide users with the best experience possible, and fast loading times contribute to this goal. For reference, a Backlinko study found that the first page of Google results has an average load time of 1.65 seconds. The bottom line? The quicker your site loads, the more organic visibility and traffic you’ll likely receive. Increased Conversion Rates Better load times can also improve your conversion rates. Visitors are less likely to become frustrated with the on-page experience and abandon your site. A 2022 Portent study found that an ecommerce site that loads in one second has a 2.5 times higher conversion rate than a site that takes five seconds to load. Likewise, a 2017 Akamai study found that a mere 100-millisecond delay in mobile page speed can reduce conversions by 7%. 5 Causes of Sluggish Websites (And How to Fix Them) Now, let’s look at the main culprits behind slow-loading websites and what to do about them. 1. Large Image and Media Files Large media files—like images, videos, and GIFs—can take up a lot of bandwidth and lead to slower page speeds. This happen [Excerpt truncated at 3,000 characters]

22 small business tax deductions for your return in 2025 By Jess Holy Updated: April 28, 2025 Average reading time: 11 minutes Share this article: There are a number of business tax deductions that can help you save money. Learn about which deductions your small business could be eligible for this year. Taxes are a fact of life for small business owners. But tax deductions can help you keep more of the money you work so hard to earn. No matter what your business does or what industry you’re in, there are plenty of tax deductions available to claim. You might be surprised to learn that simple business expenses like your cellphone bill or your new computer can be deducted from your taxable income. In fact, there are some fully-deductible expenses such as advertising and marketing costs, employee education and training, and certain legal fees. Additionally, some vital deductions are set to expire at the end of 2025, such as the 20% Small Business Deduction, which your business may need to plan for in the coming year. Use this checklist to determine what small business tax deductions you can take advantage of for the 2025 tax season. 1. Home office Over the last few years there's been a shift to remote work and hybrid work environments. If you operate your business out of a home office , you are allowed to deduct the cost per square foot of your dedicated office space. According to the IRS , taxpayers who work from home can deduct $5 per square foot of space that is used as a home office, up to 300 square feet. That equals a maximum deduction of $1,500. The home office deduction includes the cost of utilities, such as heat, electricity, and Wi-Fi, so those expenses can’t be deducted separately. To qualify for the home office deduction for 2024, your workspace must have met the following three requirements: The workspace has clearly defined boundaries. It should be a separate room or portion of a room. That means working from your bedroom doesn't count. It must be the regular, or normal, place where you work. If you typically work at a nearby co-working space, but you work from your kitchen table occasionally, you probably couldn't claim the deduction. You must engage in regular and important business from your home office. For example, if you are a medical provider who sees patients in a hospital, but you do paperwork and billing at home, that likely would not qualify. 2. Office supplies Whether you work from home or an office, you need certain items to keep your business running. That’s why the IRS allows small business owners to write off the cost of office supplies from their taxes. Examples of office supplies that qualify as deductible business expenses include: Printer paper Markers, pencils, and other writing utensils Computer software that is essential to your business 3. Business insurance premiums Having the correct small business insurance is key to maintaining the financial health of your company. Some types of business insurance are requi [Excerpt truncated at 3,000 characters]

Enhancing Safety and Comfort: A Guide to Automotive User Experience Design January 25, 2025 November 1, 2024 by Wheels & Motion Editorial AI Disclaimer: Some parts of this content were generated by AI. Verify critical facts. As the automotive industry rapidly evolves, Automotive User Experience Design has emerged as a critical factor in shaping the future of car technologies. This design discipline focuses on creating seamless interactions between drivers and vehicles, enhancing safety and satisfaction. The integration of innovative features in this domain not only enriches user experiences but also responds effectively to the growing demand for connectivity and autonomy in vehicles. Attention to user-centered design is paramount for manufacturers aiming to remain competitive in this dynamic landscape. Table of Contents Toggle The Evolution of Automotive User Experience Design Automotive User Experience Design has evolved significantly over the decades, transitioning from basic functionality to a sophisticated integration of technology and human-centered design. Initially, car interiors were focused on mechanical controls and minimal user interaction, as vehicles were primarily utilitarian. As advancements in technology emerged, the introduction of digital displays and infotainment systems transformed the automotive landscape. Companies began prioritizing user engagement, leading to the incorporation of touchscreens and voice-activated commands, enhancing overall driver comfort and satisfaction. In recent years, the trend has shifted towards seamless connectivity and personalization. Features such as smartphone integration and advanced driver-assistance systems have become standard, reflecting the growing emphasis on tailoring user experiences. The evolution of Automotive User Experience Design now plays a pivotal role in shaping future car technologies. Importance of User-Centered Design in Future Car Technologies User-centered design prioritizes the needs and preferences of users, making it a fundamental aspect of automotive user experience design. In the rapidly evolving landscape of future car technologies, this approach fosters greater safety, efficiency, and satisfaction among drivers and passengers alike. By focusing on user behaviors and interactions, manufacturers can ensure that features such as infotainment systems, controls, and displays are intuitive and accessible. Key benefits of implementing user-centered design include: Enhanced usability and ergonomics. Increased driver confidence and safety. Improved overall user satisfaction. As vehicles transition towards advanced technologies, including electric and autonomous vehicles, understanding user expectations becomes paramount. Effective user-centered design can bridge the gap between technology and user interaction, facilitating a seamless driving experience that aligns with modern demands and preferences. 06 / Brainstorm

A small, inspectable improvement loop.

Starting direction: a query-aware page improvement assistant. Accept a query, existing page, and approved factual material; identify coverage gaps; produce a revised title, outline, and section; explain which observations informed the edits.

Two alternatives

Content blueprint generator: create a source-grounded draft for one query class, with structure conditional on page purpose.

Evidence explorer with generation: inspect matched examples, change filters, choose a hypothesis, and generate a draft that applies it.

What a good demo proves

It completes the evidence-to-draft workflow, addresses the query, traces recommendations to the analysis, and adds no unsupported facts. Citation uplift needs a separate prospective experiment.

Clarify label construction, engine and time coverage, citation counts and exposure, snapshot timing, and the factual sources available for generation.

Methods & provenance

Content analysis uses one page per hostname and URL (fragments removed), picks the longest snapshot where versions differ, and excludes conflicting labels. Text extraction selects the largest main/article/role=main container, falling back to a cleaned body. It is not a rendered-browser view. Quality checks are conservative text heuristics, not verified HTTP status or MIME data.

Exploratory feature tests and multiple-testing adjustments are recorded in the source results. Multiple sensitivity runs and unmeasured confounding limit interpretation. No held-out prediction study or prospective citation experiment has been performed.

Source results: schema audit · quality audit · content analysis · sensitivity analysis · sample manifest · archive provenance. Rebuild from the project root: .tools/uv run python scripts/build_report.py.

Prepared from the supplied citation dataset · Analysis date: 01 October 2026 · Offline report; no external assets or telemetry.
